<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We construct, in some sufficiently large fixed finite complex dimension <i>m</i>, a domain in ℂ<sup><i>m</i></sup> diffeomorphic to $`\mathbb R^{2m}`$ that admits a complete Kähler metric with real sectional curvature at most −1 and has only constant bounded holomorphic functions. Its sectional curvatures are unbounded below. The construction gives a negative answer to the one-sided bounded-holomorphic-function question.

- **claim-main-liouville-counterexample** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For some finite integer $m\ge2$ there is a domain $\mathcal T\subset\mathbb C^m$, diffeomorphic to $\mathbb R^{2m}$, with a complete Kähler metric $g$ such that $$\operatorname{Sec}_g\le-1, \qquad H^\infty(\mathcal T)=\mathbb C.$$ The sectional curvatures of $g$ are unbounded below.
  - Explanation: In a sufficiently large fixed finite complex dimension there is a domain diffeomorphic to real Euclidean space with a complete Kähler metric whose sectional curvatures are bounded above by a negative constant, while every bounded holomorphic function is constant. The curvature is unbounded below. This answers the one-sided question negatively.
- **claim-tube-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-tube-transfer)): Let $\ell\colon\mathbb C^{n+1}\to\mathbb R$ be smooth and strictly plurisubharmonic. Suppose that its potential metric $g_\ell$, in Convention <a href="#tube:metric-convention" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="tube:metric-convention">[tube:metric-convention]</a>, is complete and has nonpositive real sectional curvature. Set $$\label{tube:domain} \mathcal T_\ell =\{(z,w)\in\mathbb C^{n+1}\times\mathbb C: b(z,w):=\operatorname{Im}w-\ell(z)>0\}, \qquad g_{\mathcal T}=g_{-\log b}.$$ Then $\mathcal T_\ell$ is connected and simply connected, indeed diffeomorphic to $\mathbb R^{2n+4}$, and $g_{\mathcal T}$ is a complete Kähler metric satisfying $$\operatorname{Sec}_{g_{\mathcal T}}\le -1.$$ No lower curvature bound on $g_\ell$ is required.
  - Explanation: A complete nonpositively curved Kähler potential metric on complex Euclidean space yields a tube domain over it carrying a complete Kähler metric with sectional curvature at most minus one. No lower curvature bound on the base is required.
- **claim-tube-negative-plane** (theorem, [source](#stmt-tube-negative-plane)): Under the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#tube:transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tube:transfer">2</a>, suppose that $g_\ell$ has a real two-plane of strictly negative sectional curvature at some point. Then $$\inf\operatorname{Sec}_{g_{\mathcal T}}=-\infty.$$
  - Explanation: If the base metric has a plane of strictly negative curvature somewhere, the tube metric has sectional curvatures unbounded below, because the base contribution scales with the height.
- **claim-grid-polynomials** (theorem, [source](#stmt-grid-polynomials)): Fix $n\ge2$. For every sufficiently large integer $N$ one can choose a system of homogeneous polynomials of degree $N$, $$Q_N=(H_N,p_N):\mathbb C^n\longrightarrow\mathbb C^{n-1}\times\mathbb C,$$ with the following properties. These choices can be made along all integers $N\to\infty$, or along any prescribed sequence of degrees tending to infinity.* 1. *There is a constant $C_n$ such that $$\label{grid:sphere-bounds} N^{-C_n}\le \|Q_N(u)\|\le N\qquad (\|u\|=1).$$* 2. *The projective zero set of $H_N$ consists of $M_N=N^{n-1}$ transverse points. A subset $\mathcal G_N$ of these points satisfies $$\|\mathcal G_N\|=(1-o(1))M_N, \qquad \frac1{M_N}\sum_{\xi\in\mathcal G_N}\delta_\xi \ \rightharpoonup\ \sigma.$$ For every $\xi\in\mathcal G_N$ and every unit representative $u$ of $\xi$, $$\label{grid:goodness} N^{-1}\le \|p_N(u)\|\le N, \qquad \operatorname{sing}_{\min} \bigl(DH_N(u)\|_{u^\perp}\bigr)\ge N^{-1}.$$* 3. *Above each $\xi\in\mathcal G_N$, choose any lift $v_\xi$ such that $p_N(v_\xi)=1$. All $N$ lifts $$e^{2\pi i q/N}v_\xi,\qquad 0\le q<N,$$ have radii in $[N^{-1/N},N^{1/N}]$. With the absolute choice $k_*=30$, the Euclidean balls of radius $2N^{-k_*}$ about all these lifts are pairwise disjoint. If $v$ is one of the lifts, then $$\label{grid:shell-bound} \|(H_N(z),p_N(z)-1)\|\ge N^{-k_*-10} \quad\hbox{when}\quad N^{-k_*}\le \|z-v\|\le2N^{-k_*}.$$ In a neighborhood of radius $4N^{-k_*}$ of every such lift, the first and second derivatives of $Q_N$ have norm $O_n(N^5)$.* *In particular, weak approximation to any prescribed finite collection of continuous test functions, with any fixed positive accuracy, can be required by taking $N$ sufficiently large.
  - Explanation: For all large degrees there are homogeneous polynomial systems whose projective zero sets are transverse, equidistribute in the limit, and admit cyclic affine lifts with pairwise disjoint balls and shell lower bounds.
- **claim-grid-phases** (theorem, [source](#stmt-grid-phases)): Fix positive integers $k,n$, with $n\ge2$, such that $$\label{grid:dimension-choice} \sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\frac{j^{n-1}}{(n-1)!}<\frac1{100}.$$ Fix a nonnegative integer $h$. For sufficiently large polynomial grids from Lemma <a href="#grid:polynomials" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="grid:polynomials">4</a>, choose one lift $v_\xi$ above each good direction and let $V_N\subset\mathbb C^{\mathcal G_N}$ be the space of evaluation vectors of sums of homogeneous polynomials of degrees $$h,\ h+N,\ \ldots,\ h+(k-1)N.$$ Let $\Pi_N$ be orthogonal projection onto $V_N$ for the unweighted Euclidean point norm. There are phases $b_\xi$, $\|b_\xi\|=1$, and a constant $\delta_h>0$, independent of $N$ and of the chosen lift phases, such that $$\label{grid:phase-lower-bound} M_N^{-1/2} \left\\|(1-\Pi_N) \bigl(b_\xi P(v_\xi)\bigr)_{\xi\in\mathcal G_N}\right\\| \ge\delta_h\\|P\\|$$ for every homogeneous polynomial $P$ of degree $h$. Here $\\|P\\|$ is any fixed Euclidean norm on its coefficient space, chosen independently of the grid. All required grid accuracies and degree thresholds depend only on the fixed $n,k,h$ and this norm.
  - Explanation: Phases attached to the projective directions keep any prescribed low-degree homogeneous polynomial uniformly away from the space of evaluation vectors of the surviving Taylor degrees, with a bound independent of the lift phases.
- **claim-grid-frequency** (theorem, [source](#stmt-grid-frequency)): Let $0\le h<N$, and suppose $g$ is holomorphic on a neighborhood of the closed ball $\|s\|\le S$, with $\|g\|\le K$ there. Write $g=\sum_{r\ge0}g_r$ for its homogeneous Taylor expansion at zero. If $R\|v\|<S$, then $$\frac1N\sum_{q=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\pi i hq/N} g(Re^{2\pi i q/N}v) =\sum_{j\ge0}g_{h+jN}(Rv).$$ If $R\|v\|/S\le e^{-0.99c/N}$ and $c\ge1$, truncating the sum after $j=k-1$ has error at most $C K e^{-0.99kc}$, while retaining just the term $g_h(Rv)$ has error at most $C K e^{-0.99c}$. The constant $C$ is absolute, independent of $N,h,R,S$.
  - Explanation: A finite Fourier average over a root-of-unity cycle isolates the Taylor degrees in one residue class modulo the cycle length, with exponentially small truncation error when the evaluation radius is sufficiently inside the ball of holomorphy.
- **claim-grid-criterion** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-grid-criterion)): Fix constants $p_0,p_1\ge0$ and $m_0>p_1$. Choose an integer $k$ and a finite dimension $n\ge2$ so that $$\label{grid:exponent-choice} 0.99k>p_0+p_1+1, \qquad \sum_{r=1}^{k-1}\frac{r^{n-1}}{(n-1)!}<0.01.$$ Let $(\zeta_j,h_j)$ be a sequence of pairs from a countable dense subset of an open disk in $\mathbb C$ and the nonnegative integers, with every pair repeated infinitely often. At stage $j$, choose numbers $R_j\ge1$, $c_j\ge1$, an integer $N_j>h_j$, and a grid from Lemma <a href="#grid:polynomials" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="grid:polynomials">4</a> sufficiently large and accurate for Lemma <a href="#grid:phases" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="grid:phases">5</a> at order $h_j$. Set $$S_j=R_j e^{c_j/N_j},\qquad \log N_j\le c_j/100.$$ Choose heights $D_j$ and positive numbers $\alpha_j$ such that $$\alpha_j^{-1}\le e^{p_1c_j},$$ and, along the repetitions of every fixed pair, $$\label{grid:height-rates} c_j\longrightarrow\infty,\qquad D_j\longrightarrow\infty,\qquad \frac{D_j}{c_j}\longrightarrow0.$$ There are phases $b_{j,\xi}$, one per good projective direction and constant throughout its cycle of lifts, with the following property.* *Let $\ell$ be a smooth real function on $\mathbb C^n\times\mathbb C$, and let $$\mathcal T=\{(s,y,w):\operatorname{Im}w>\ell(s,y)\}.$$ Suppose every stage satisfies these conditions:* 1. *On $\|s\|\le S_j$, $$\label{grid:height-gap} \ell(s,\zeta_j)\le D_j-2.$$* 2. *For every holomorphic $f$ on $\mathcal T$ with $\|f\|\le1$, every $w$ with $\operatorname{Im}w=D_j$, and every $\|s\|=S_j$, $$\label{grid:sphere-derivative} \|f_y(s,\zeta_j,w)\|\le e^{p_0c_j}.$$* 3. *For every good direction $\xi$, every lift $v=e^{2\pi iq/N_j}v_\xi$, and every $w$ on that height line, the holomorphic disk $$\label{grid:sheared-disk} t\longmapsto \bigl(R_jv,\ \zeta_j+t,\ w+i\alpha_j b_{j,\xi}t\bigr), \qquad \|t\|<e^{m_0c_j},$$ is contained in $\mathcal T$.* *Then every bounded holomorphic function on $\mathcal T$ is constant. The phases depend only on the grids and their assigned jet orders, not on $\ell$, $f$, or $w$.
  - Explanation: If a smooth potential satisfies a height bound, a derivative bound on boundary spheres and containment of long sheared disks at the polynomial nodes at every stage, then every bounded holomorphic function on its tube is constant.
- **claim-fiber-metric** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fiber-metric)): Write $$P=\tau q,\qquad V=(B_{i\bar j}),\qquad G=(\psi_{i\bar j}),\qquad h=(U_{i\bar j}),\qquad \mathcal N=h_\tau.$$ Where $h>0$, the complex Hessian of $\ell_{\mathrm{rad}}$ is $$\label{fiber:metric-form} h_{i\bar j}\,ds_i\,d\bar s_j+ P\left\|d\log(y-\zeta)+U_{\tau i}\,ds_i\right\|^2.$$ It is positive off the axis. Its spatial Schur complement is exactly $h$. The potential and its complex Hessian extend smoothly across $y=\zeta$; on that axis the Hessian in the original splitting is $V\oplus e^\psi$, and is positive whenever $V>0$.
  - Explanation: The partial Legendre transform gives an explicit Hessian splitting into a spatial Schur complement and a fiber term, positive off the axis, and the potential and metric extend smoothly across the fiber axis.
- **claim-fiber-blocks** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fiber-blocks)): In tilted logarithmic coordinates, the tensor <a href="#fiber:curvature-convention" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fiber:curvature-convention">[fiber:curvature-convention]</a> has the following blocks: $$\begin{aligned} A:=C_{0\bar0 0\bar0} &=P^2P_{\tau\tau}-\|\partial P\|_h^2, \label{fiber:blockA}\\ A_i:=C_{i\bar0 0\bar0} &=P\bigl(\partial_iP_\tau- (\mathcal N h^{-1}\partial P)_i\bigr), \label{fiber:blockAi}\\ W_{i\bar j}:=C_{i\bar j0\bar0} &=P\bigl(\partial_i\partial_{\bar j}\log P +P_\tau\mathcal N_{i\bar j} -P(\mathcal N h^{-1}\mathcal N)_{i\bar j}\bigr), \label{fiber:blockW}\\ C_{i\bar0 k\bar0}&=(\nabla_h^{2,0}P)_{ik}, \label{fiber:blockspin}\\ K_{ik\bar j}:=C_{i\bar j k\bar0} &=P\nabla_i^h\mathcal N_{k\bar j} +P_i\mathcal N_{k\bar j}+P_k\mathcal N_{i\bar j}, \label{fiber:blockK}\\ L_{i\bar j k\bar l}:=C_{i\bar j k\bar l} &=C^h_{i\bar j k\bar l} +P(\mathcal N_{i\bar j}\mathcal N_{k\bar l} +\mathcal N_{i\bar l}\mathcal N_{k\bar j}). \label{fiber:blockL} \end{aligned}$$ Here $C^h$ and $\nabla^h$ are the curvature with sign <a href="#fiber:curvature-convention" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="fiber:curvature-convention">[fiber:curvature-convention]</a> and the Kähler connection of the fixed-$\tau$ spatial metric. Index positions in the products use the indicated Hermitian metric, with the usual conjugations.
  - Explanation: In tilted logarithmic coordinates the negative Hermitian curvature tensor of the fiber metric splits into six explicit blocks in terms of the profile and the spatial metric data.
- **claim-fiber-ranktwo** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fiber-ranktwo)): If $C(H,H)\ge0$ for every Hermitian contravariant matrix $H$ of rank at most two, the underlying real sectional curvatures are nonpositive. Write the spatial, cross, and fiber diagonal blocks of $H$ as $T,b_\times,t$. The contraction has the terms $$\label{fiber:contraction} At^2+2t(W:T)+2W(b_\times,\bar b_\times)+L(T,T),$$ and its other terms have absolute value at most $$\label{fiber:otherterms} 2\|\nabla_h^{2,0}P(b_\times,b_\times)\| +4\|tA_i b_\times^i\|+4\|(K:T)b_\times\|.$$ In particular the following quantitative criterion suffices. Suppose $A>0$, $W>0$, and a nonnegative quadratic form $\mathcal R(T)$ satisfies $$\begin{aligned} L(T,T)-\frac{\|W:T\|^2}{.98A}&\ge\mathcal R(T), \label{fiber:reserve}\\ \|\nabla_h^{2,0}P(b,b)\|&\le\tfrac14 W(b,\bar b), \label{fiber:spinabs}\\ \|A_i b^i\|&\le\eta\sqrt{A W(b,\bar b)}, \label{fiber:Aiabs}\\ \|(K:T)b\|&\le\eta\sqrt{\mathcal R(T)W(b,\bar b)}, \label{fiber:Kabs} \end{aligned}$$ for all such tests, where $\eta\le1/100$. Then $$\label{fiber:retained} C(H,H)\ge .01At^2+W(b_\times,\bar b_\times) +\tfrac12\mathcal R(T).$$ All constants are independent of dimension.
  - Explanation: Nonnegativity of the curvature contraction on Hermitian matrices of low rank implies nonpositive real sectional curvature, and a quantitative absorption criterion retains margins independent of dimension.
- **claim-fiber-perturbation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fiber-perturbation)): In any fixed holomorphic coordinates let $H$ denote the cubic jet viewed as a map from two tangent inputs to a covector, and write $H=\Gamma g$. For a perturbation with $g+\Delta g>0$, the change of the cubic contraction $Hg^{-1}H^*$ is exactly $$\label{fiber:perturbformula} \Gamma\Delta H^*+\Delta H\Gamma^*-\Gamma\Delta g\Gamma^* +(\Delta H-\Gamma\Delta g)(g+\Delta g)^{-1} (\Delta H-\Gamma\Delta g)^*.$$ Consequently, if $\\|g^{-1}\Delta g\\|$ in metric-unit axes is at most one half, the inverse of the perturbed metric is at most twice that of $g$, and $$\begin{aligned} \label{fiber:perturbbound} \\|\Delta C\\|\le {}&\\|\Delta j^4\ell\\| +2\\|\Gamma\\|\\|\Delta H\\| +\\|\Gamma\\|^2\\|\Delta g\\|\\ &+2\\|g^{-1}\\| (\\|\Delta H\\|+\\|\Gamma\\|\\|\Delta g\\|)^2. \notag \end{aligned}$$ The same bound, with an absolute factor, controls rank-two contractions.
  - Explanation: The change of the cubic contraction under a metric perturbation has an exact algebraic expression, which yields operator-norm bounds on the change of the curvature tensor.
- **claim-quiet-scalar** (theorem, [source](#stmt-quiet-scalar)): Put $$q_a=(1+e^Eu)^{b_0},\quad r=r_q=q_x/q,\quad D=D_q=(q_x+q_{xx})/q=r(1+r)+r_x,\quad e_0=q_E/q.$$ For all sufficiently large $E$ and all $\tau>0$, $$\begin{gathered} q\ge1,\quad q_x>0,\quad D\ge r(r+\tfrac12),\quad q_{xx}/q_x\ge-\tfrac1{16}, \label{quiet:slopes}\\ 0<r\le C,\quad \|e_0\|+\|q_{EE}/q\|\le C, \quad \|r_E\|\le Cr. \label{quiet:derivatives} \end{gathered}$$ For $\tau\le1$, the first two quantities in the middle of <a href="#quiet:derivatives" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="quiet:derivatives">[quiet:derivatives]</a> are also at most $Cr$. For $1\le j\le4$, $$\label{quiet:Fderivatives} \|\partial_E^jF\|+\|\partial_E^j\bar F\|\le C, \qquad \bar F(\tau,E)=\tau^{-1}\int_0^\tau F(\log v,E)\,dv.$$ For $0\le j\le4$ and $\tau\ge e^{-E/4}$, $$\label{quiet:difference} \|\partial_E^j(F-\bar F)\|\le C e^{-E/4},\qquad \|\partial_E^j(F-\bar F)\|\le Cr.$$ The following additional estimates will be used: $$\begin{aligned} q_x&\ge e^E &&(e^{-E/4}\le\tau\le e^E), \label{quiet:intermediate}\\ q&\le C(e^{b_0E}+\log(1+\tau)),\qquad q_x\ge c(1+e^{b_0E}(1+\tau)^{-1/16}) &&(\tau\ge1), \label{quiet:tail}\\ r&\ge .9 b_0(1+\tau)^{-1/16} &&(1\le\tau\le e^{3b_0E}), \label{quiet:middle-slope}\\ q&\le C,\quad q-1\le Ce^E\tau,\quad r\asymp e^E\tau,\quad D\asymp e^E\tau &&(0<\tau\le2e^{-E}). \label{quiet:axis-slope} \end{aligned}$$ In particular $q_x\ge c$ for $\tau\ge e^{-E}$, and $q^{-1}\le Cr$ for $\tau\ge2e^{-E}$.
  - Explanation: The explicit profile has uniform slope, derivative and difference estimates in the severity parameter across all fiber momenta once the severity is large.
- **claim-quiet-profile** (theorem, [source](#stmt-quiet-profile)): There exist positive constants $\epsilon,\mu$ and a lower threshold $E_{\min}$, depending on the fixed profile constants but not on $n$, with the following property. Let $E$ be a smooth spatial function and use $q=q_0(x,E(s))$ with constant center and no shear. At a spatial point suppose, in some fixed holomorphic affine coordinates, that:* 1. *$G\ge V>0$. The operator norms of $V,G$, their inverses, their first and second metric jets, and all derivatives of $E$ of orders one through four are at most $e^{\epsilon E}$.* 2. *For every Hermitian contravariant matrix $T$ of rank at most two, $C^V(T,T)\ge e^{-\epsilon E}\|T\|^2$, with the norm in those affine coordinates.* 3. *In $G$-unit axes, $$\|\partial E\|_G^2\le\mu,\qquad \\|\partial\bar\partial E\\|_G\le\mu,\qquad \\|\nabla_h^{2,0}E\\|_G\le\mu \quad\hbox{for every }\tau>0.$$* *If $E\ge E_{\min}$, the resulting metric is positive for every $y$ over that spatial point and has nonpositive real sectional curvature there. Uniformly in $\tau$, $$\label{quiet:schur} \mathcal N=(1+O(\mu))G,\qquad h\asymp V+\tau G.$$ More precisely, off the fiber axis the rank-two curvature calculation retains fixed positive multiples of $$\label{quiet:margins} At^2+W(b_\times,\bar b_\times)+P\|T\|_{\mathcal N}^2 +\boldsymbol{1}_{\{\tau\le e^{-E/4}\}} e^{-\epsilon E}\|T\|^2.$$ Here the fiber blocks use the tilted logarithmic coordinates, $\|T\|_{\mathcal N}$ is its Frobenius norm in $\mathcal N$-unit spatial axes, and $\boldsymbol{1}$ denotes the indicated indicator. The meaning of the final hypothesis is noncircular: positivity of $h$ follows from the preceding hypotheses and does not use $\nabla_h^{2,0}E$.
  - Explanation: With small spatial derivatives of the severity and a positive spatial curvature reserve, the profile metric is positive with nonpositive real sectional curvature, with retained fixed margins independent of dimension.
- **claim-quiet-shear** (theorem, [source](#stmt-quiet-shear)): Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#quiet:profile" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="quiet:profile">15</a>, suppose $E$ and the center are constant on the spatial region under consideration. Assume also that the first and pure second spatial derivatives of $\psi$, in the affine coordinates of that proposition, have norms at most $e^{\epsilon E}$. Add $$\ell_{\mathrm{sh}}=\operatorname{Re}(c_{\mathrm{sh}}(s)(y-\zeta)).$$ On the support of its coefficient’s derivatives suppose $\psi\ge-J$, where $J\ge0$, and $$\label{quiet:shear-small} \\|j^{\le4}c_{\mathrm{sh}}\\|\le e^{-C E-J/2}.$$ For a sufficiently large $C$ depending only on the profile and the constants in the quiet criterion, this perturbation preserves metric positivity and nonpositive real sectional curvature for all $y$. Moreover its metric is at least one half the unperturbed metric, and fixed fractions of the curvature margins remain. The choice of $C$ is independent of dimension.
  - Explanation: A localized shear term linear in the fiber variable, with sufficiently small spatial jets, preserves positivity and nonpositive real sectional curvature on all fiber radii and keeps fixed fractions of the margins.
- **claim-radial-odes** (theorem, [source](#stmt-radial-odes)): Suppose $a,a_\psi,Y>0$, $\lambda\ge1$, and $$\label{radial:kappa-assumptions} \frac1{1+Y}\le\kappa\le1.1,\qquad \frac{d\kappa}{d\log Y}\ge-\kappa.$$ Then $$\begin{aligned} \label{radial:clock-equations} \frac{d\nu}{du}&=\frac1Y,& \frac{d\log Y}{du}&=\kappa(1+1/Y),& \frac{d\log\lambda}{du}&=1-p,\\ \frac{d\Psi}{du}&=\frac1H,& \frac{d\log H}{du}&=\kappa(1+1/Y)-p-1/Y.\notag \end{aligned}$$ If $Y\ge t$ initially, then $Y\ge t$ subsequently. With smooth bounded controls $p,\kappa$ on each finite $u$ interval, positive solutions have no blowup on that interval. A cap of the form $\kappa=C_\kappa/(1+Y)$, with fixed $C_\kappa\ge1$, has no blowup at finite $\nu$ for the prescribed bounded controls.* *If $p=1$, then $$\label{radial:H-logY} \frac{d\log H}{d\log Y}=1-\frac1\kappa.$$ In particular, $p=\kappa=1$ holds both $\lambda$ and $H$ constant. If $\kappa\ge1$ on an interval beginning at $u_0$, then the total increase of $\nu$ on that interval is at most $1/Y(u_0)$, even if its finite $u$ length is arbitrarily large.
  - Explanation: The radial clock variables obey an explicit first-order system; positive solutions with bounded controls do not blow up on finite intervals, and capped growth continues without a pole.
- **claim-radial-curvature** (theorem, [source](#stmt-radial-curvature)): Under <a href="#radial:kappa-assumptions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="radial:kappa-assumptions">[radial:kappa-assumptions]</a>, the negative Hermitian curvature tensor $C^V=-R^V$ satisfies $$\label{radial:rank-reserve} C^V(T,T)\ge c\,\kappa\frac{H}{\lambda}\|T\|_V^2 \qquad\text{if }T=T^*,\quad\operatorname{rank}T\le2,$$ where $c>0$ is absolute. Here the subscript $V$ means that the matrix is expressed in $V$-unit axes.
  - Explanation: The radial spatial metric has a curvature reserve on Hermitian matrices of low rank proportional to the clock quantities, with an absolute constant.
- **claim-radial-scaled** (theorem, [source](#stmt-radial-scaled)): Assume <a href="#radial:kappa-assumptions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="radial:kappa-assumptions">[radial:kappa-assumptions]</a> and $\lambda\ge1$. Suppose $Y\ge1$, $Y\ge t$, the first two derivatives of $\kappa$ in $\log Y$ are bounded by a fixed constant, and $$\label{radial:p-close} \|p-1\|+\left\|\frac{dp}{d\log Y}\right\| +\left\|\frac{d^2p}{d(\log Y)^2}\right\|\le\delta, \qquad 0<\delta\le\tfrac1{10}.$$ At a point use $G_0$-unit affine axes divided by $\sqrt H$. In these axes $G_0=H^{-1}I$ at the point. The transverse and radial Euclidean axis lengths are $$\label{radial:axis-lengths} \sqrt{\frac tY},\qquad \sqrt{\frac{t}{Y(1+pY)}}.$$ In these fixed coordinates:* 1. *derivatives of $\Psi$ and $B$ in orders one through four are bounded by $C/H$ and $C\lambda/H$, respectively;* 2. *metric jets through order two of $V-\lambda G_0$, with $\lambda$ frozen at its value at the point, are bounded by $C\delta\lambda/H$;* 3. *derivatives of $u$ in orders one through four are bounded by $C$;* 4. *if a further potential has Euclidean derivatives in orders two through four bounded by $D_e$, its Hessian and first two metric jets here are bounded by $CD_e/(a_\psi H)$.* *All constants are independent of dimension.
  - Explanation: In normalized affine axes the potentials, their metric jets and the logarithmic variable have uniform derivative bounds independent of dimension.
- **claim-radial-aligned** (theorem, [source](#stmt-radial-aligned)): There is an absolute $\delta_0>0$ with the following property. Fix $\sigma>0$ and take the severity $E$ sufficiently large in terms of $\sigma$ and the fixed profile. Assume the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#radial:scaled" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="radial:scaled">19</a>, with $\delta\le\delta_0$, and also $$\tfrac12\le\kappa\le1.1,\qquad H\ge\sigma, \qquad G=G_0+G_e,\qquad \|\partial^{[j]}G_e\|\le\delta/H\quad(0\le j\le2)$$ in its scaled affine axes. Here $G=\partial\bar\partial\psi$ and $\partial^{[j]}$ denotes any real or complex metric jet of order $j$, measured as a multilinear operator. Suppose either $$\label{radial:aligned-alternatives} \lambda=1,\qquad\text{or}\qquad H\ge M_E(1+\log\lambda)^2,$$ where $M_E$ is sufficiently large depending only on the fixed profile and $E$. Then the constant-center fiber potential defines a positive metric with nonpositive real sectional curvature. Its spatial Schur complement satisfies $h\ge cV$.* *More precisely, in scaled spatial axes and the tilted logarithmic fiber coordinate of Lemma <a href="#fiber:blocks" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fiber:blocks">11</a>, the contraction of its negative curvature on each Hermitian rank-at-most-two matrix with blocks $(T,b,t_f)$ is bounded below by $$\label{radial:aligned-margin} c\left(A t_f^2+W(b,\bar b) +\frac{\lambda+\tau}{H}\|T\|^2 +P\|T\|_G^2\right),$$ where $c>0$ can be fixed independently of dimension and the stage parameters once the stated thresholds hold. The coefficient $A$ and the form $W$ are those of Lemma <a href="#fiber:blocks" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fiber:blocks">11</a>.
  - Explanation: When the two spatial metrics are aligned and the clock quantities lie in the stated ranges, the constant-center fiber potential has positive metric, nonpositive real sectional curvature and Schur complement bounded below by the spatial metric.
- **claim-center-change** (theorem, [source](#stmt-center-change)): Choose the profile constants in the following order: first the fixed exponent $b_0$ of the quiet profile; then a sufficiently large absolute constant $d_0$; and then $K_0$ sufficiently large in terms of $d_0$ and absolute constants specified in the proof. Fix $E$ and old data as in <a href="#center:old-data" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="center:old-data">[center:old-data]</a>. There is a width parameter $L$, chosen using only these data and the fixed profile, such that the following construction is possible.* *After choosing $L$, choose a sufficiently large $\lambda$, and then a sufficiently large $H$; explicitly one can require $$\label{center:target-order} \lambda\ge\max\{e^{3L},\Lambda_f\}, \qquad H\ge M_f(1+\log\lambda)^2,$$ where $\Lambda_f,M_f$ are finite constants determined by the fixed profile, $E,L$, the old data, and $\delta$, but not by $\lambda,H$. Over three fixed units of the variable $\Psi$, one can change from any prescribed constant center in the unit disk to any other such center, returning to the original profile $q_0(x,E)$ at the end. The final twist differs from the old twist by a nonnegative constant, possibly very large.* *During the change the potential is smooth, its metric is positive and has nonpositive real sectional curvature, and the metric bounds $cV$ from below on spatial projection, for a fixed $c>0$. There is no restriction on the cost of the final constant offset. All power exponents and smallness tolerances used before choosing the spatial dimension are dimension independent.
  - Explanation: After widening the fiber profile, the center can be moved between any two prescribed centers in the unit disk and the profile restored, at the cost of a large nonnegative constant offset, preserving positivity and nonpositive curvature.
- **claim-center-interpolation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-center-interpolation)): During either profile switch above, keep the center constant and set $\psi=\psi_{\rm old}+w^\pm(\beta(\Psi))$, also including the already frozen offset when making the reverse switch. If $\lambda$ and then $H$ satisfy sufficiently large thresholds of the form <a href="#center:target-order" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="center:target-order">[center:target-order]</a>, the metric is positive, $h\ge cV$, and its negative curvature has a strictly positive rank-two reserve off the fiber axis. These conclusions extend across that axis by continuity.
  - Explanation: During the forward and reverse profile switches the metric stays positive, bounded below by the spatial metric, with a strictly positive rank-restricted curvature reserve off the axis, extended across the axis by continuity.
- **claim-assembly-base** (theorem, [source](#stmt-assembly-base)): For some fixed finite integer $n$, there is a smooth strictly plurisubharmonic potential $\ell$ on $\mathbb C^n\times\mathbb C$ whose Kähler metric is complete and has nonpositive real sectional curvature. The potential satisfies every hypothesis of Proposition <a href="#grid:criterion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="grid:criterion">8</a>, for a countable dense set of centers in the unit disk and every spatial Taylor order. Thus all bounded holomorphic functions on its half-plane tube are constant.
  - Explanation: There is a complete nonpositively curved plurisubharmonic base potential in a fixed finite dimension satisfying the testing criterion, so all bounded holomorphic functions on its tube are constant.
- **claim-assembly-well-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-assembly-well-bounds)): There is an absolute exponent $C_{\rm pol}$ such that, for $t\geq1$ and $1\leq r\leq4$, $$\label{assembly:well-raw} \|\partial^{[r]}W_j\| \leq C(n,d)\exp\{C_{\rm pol}(\max(l,0)+c+\log N)\}.$$ There is a constant $L_n<\infty$ such that, after increasing the degree threshold, $$\label{assembly:well-tail} \|\partial^{[r]}W_j\|\leq N^{L_n} \qquad(1\leq r\leq4,\ l\geq L_n\log N).$$ Both statements hold throughout the indicated outer ranges.
  - Explanation: The logarithmic polynomial wells have jets bounded by an exponential in the parameters with an absolute exponent, and by a power of the degree far from the origin.
- **claim-assembly-height** (bound, [source](#stmt-assembly-height)): Before choosing $c$ at stage $j$, one can fix $D_j\geq j$ such that, for every sufficiently large permitted $c$ and every final choice of the reserved tails, $$\label{assembly:height-bound} \ell(s,\zeta_j)\leq D_j-2\qquad(\|s\|\leq Re^{c/N}).$$
  - Explanation: A testing height can be fixed before the accuracy parameter so that the potential on the center axis stays below it with margin, independent of later wells.
- **claim-assembly-quiet-check** (theorem, [source](#stmt-assembly-quiet-check)): The trigger satisfies the hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#quiet:profile" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="quiet:profile">15</a>, uniformly for every $\tau\geq0$. After saturation the same criterion applies through $l=4c$, including the initial part of the post-test term defined below.
  - Explanation: The severity trigger and the beginning of the post-test term satisfy the quiet criterion uniformly in the fiber momentum.
- **claim-assembly-post** (theorem, [source](#stmt-assembly-post)): The quiet criterion holds throughout the post ramp. Beyond $l=T_*+4$, the sum of all already generated additive twist terms other than $\Psi$ and the constant offsets, and each such term individually, has Euclidean positive-order jets through order four bounded on the whole outer range by $$\label{assembly:outer-bound} D_{\rm out}=\exp(\epsilon_1E_{\rm post}/3).$$
  - Explanation: The quiet criterion persists through the post-test ramp, and the accumulated additive twist terms have controlled jets on the whole outer range.
- **claim-assembly-change-order** (construction, [source](#stmt-assembly-change-order)): At constant $E_{\rm post}$ the center can be changed from $\zeta_j$ to $\zeta_{j+1}$, and the data can then be restored to $$\lambda=p=1,\qquad \kappa=1,\qquad H=\sigma,$$ using finite clock intervals and only the aligned and center-change regimes. The old-twist bounds needed for choosing the profile shift are available independently of the targeted $\lambda,H$.
  - Explanation: At constant severity the center moves to the next scheduled one and the radial data are restored, using finite clock intervals and only the aligned and center-change regimes.
- **claim-assembly-reset** (construction, [source](#stmt-assembly-reset)): From the endpoint in Lemma <a href="#assembly:change-order" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="assembly:change-order">28</a>, severity can be increased and a new cap started so that the next trigger has <a href="#assembly:trigger-initial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="assembly:trigger-initial">[assembly:trigger-initial]</a>, while the metric continues to satisfy the quiet criterion. The cap can be extended to arbitrarily large spatial radii.
  - Explanation: Severity can be raised to catch up with the spatial scale and a new capped interval started, preserving the quiet criterion and extending to arbitrarily large radii.

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-grid-polynomials",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $n\\ge2$. For every sufficiently large integer $N$ one can choose a system of homogeneous polynomials of degree $N$, $$Q_N=(H_N,p_N):\\mathbb C^n\\longrightarrow\\mathbb C^{n-1}\\times\\mathbb C,$$ with the following properties. These choices can be made along all integers $N\\to\\infty$, or along any prescribed sequence of degrees tending to infinity.*\n\n\n1.  *There is a constant $C_n$ such that $$\\label{grid:sphere-bounds}\n     N^{-C_n}\\le |Q_N(u)|\\le N\\qquad (|u|=1).$$*\n\n\n2.  *The projective zero set of $H_N$ consists of $M_N=N^{n-1}$ transverse points. A subset $\\mathcal G_N$ of these points satisfies $$|\\mathcal G_N|=(1-o(1))M_N,\n     \\qquad\n     \\frac1{M_N}\\sum_{\\xi\\in\\mathcal G_N}\\delta_\\xi\n     \\ \\rightharpoonup\\ \\sigma.$$ For every $\\xi\\in\\mathcal G_N$ and every unit representative $u$ of $\\xi$, $$\\label{grid:goodness}\n     N^{-1}\\le |p_N(u)|\\le N,\n     \\qquad\n     \\operatorname{sing}_{\\min}\n           \\bigl(DH_N(u)|_{u^\\perp}\\bigr)\\ge N^{-1}.$$*\n\n\n3.  *Above each $\\xi\\in\\mathcal G_N$, choose any lift $v_\\xi$ such that $p_N(v_\\xi)=1$. All $N$ lifts $$e^{2\\pi i q/N}v_\\xi,\\qquad 0\\le q<N,$$ have radii in $[N^{-1/N},N^{1/N}]$. With the absolute choice $k_*=30$, the Euclidean balls of radius $2N^{-k_*}$ about all these lifts are pairwise disjoint. If $v$ is one of the lifts, then $$\\label{grid:shell-bound}\n     |(H_N(z),p_N(z)-1)|\\ge N^{-k_*-10}\n     \\quad\\hbox{when}\\quad\n     N^{-k_*}\\le |z-v|\\le2N^{-k_*}.$$ In a neighborhood of radius $4N^{-k_*}$ of every such lift, the first and second derivatives of $Q_N$ have norm $O_n(N^5)$.*\n\n*In particular, weak approximation to any prescribed finite collection of continuous test functions, with any fixed positive accuracy, can be required by taking $N$ sufficiently large.",
        "source_id": "PM-359.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-grid-phases",
        "exact_quote": "Fix positive integers $k,n$, with $n\\ge2$, such that $$\\label{grid:dimension-choice}\n \\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\\frac{j^{n-1}}{(n-1)!}<\\frac1{100}.$$ Fix a nonnegative integer $h$. For sufficiently large polynomial grids from Lemma <a href=\"#grid:polynomials\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"grid:polynomials\">4</a>, choose one lift $v_\\xi$ above each good direction and let $V_N\\subset\\mathbb C^{\\mathcal G_N}$ be the space of evaluation vectors of sums of homogeneous polynomials of degrees $$h,\\ h+N,\\ \\ldots,\\ h+(k-1)N.$$ Let $\\Pi_N$ be orthogonal projection onto $V_N$ for the unweighted Euclidean point norm. There are phases $b_\\xi$, $|b_\\xi|=1$, and a constant $\\delta_h>0$, independent of $N$ and of the chosen lift phases, such that $$\\label{grid:phase-lower-bound}\n M_N^{-1/2}\n \\left\\|(1-\\Pi_N)\n       \\bigl(b_\\xi P(v_\\xi)\\bigr)_{\\xi\\in\\mathcal G_N}\\right\\|\n \\ge\\delta_h\\|P\\|$$ for every homogeneous polynomial $P$ of degree $h$. Here $\\|P\\|$ is any fixed Euclidean norm on its coefficient space, chosen independently of the grid. All required grid accuracies and degree thresholds depend only on the fixed $n,k,h$ and this norm.",
        "source_id": "PM-359.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

There are phases $b_\\xi$, $|b_\\xi|=1$, and a constant $\\delta_h>0$, independent of $N$ and of the chosen lift phases, such that $$\\label{grid:phase-lower-bound}\n M_N^{-1/2}\n \\left\\|(1-\\Pi_N)\n       \\bigl(b_\\xi P(v_\\xi)\\bigr)_{\\xi\\in\\mathcal G_N}\\right\\|\n \\ge\\delta_h\\|P\\|$$ for every homogeneous polynomial $P$ of degree $h$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-grid-frequency",
        "exact_quote": "Let $0\\le h<N$, and suppose $g$ is holomorphic on a neighborhood of the closed ball $|s|\\le S$, with $|g|\\le K$ there. Write $g=\\sum_{r\\ge0}g_r$ for its homogeneous Taylor expansion at zero. If $R|v|<S$, then $$\\frac1N\\sum_{q=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\\pi i hq/N}\n       g(Re^{2\\pi i q/N}v)\n   =\\sum_{j\\ge0}g_{h+jN}(Rv).$$ If $R|v|/S\\le e^{-0.99c/N}$ and $c\\ge1$, truncating the sum after $j=k-1$ has error at most $C K e^{-0.99kc}$, while retaining just the term $g_h(Rv)$ has error at most $C K e^{-0.99c}$. The constant $C$ is absolute, independent of $N,h,R,S$.",
        "source_id": "PM-359.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-grid-frequency",
          "exact_quote": "Let $0\\le h<N$, and suppose $g$ is holomorphic on a neighborhood of the closed ball $|s|\\le S$, with $|g|\\le K$ there. If $R|v|<S$, then $$\\frac1N\\sum_{q=0}^{N-1}e^{-2\\pi i hq/N}\n       g(Re^{2\\pi i q/N}v)\n   =\\sum_{j\\ge0}g_{h+jN}(Rv).$$ If $R|v|/S\\le e^{-0.99c/N}$ and $c\\ge1$, truncating the sum after $j=k-1$ has error at most $C K e^{-0.99kc}$, while retaining just the term $g_h(Rv)$ has error at most $C K e^{-0.99c}$. The constant $C$ is absolute, independent of $N,h,R,S$.",
          "source_id": "PM-359.2"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-grid-criterion",
        "exact_quote": "Fix constants $p_0,p_1\\ge0$ and $m_0>p_1$. Choose an integer $k$ and a finite dimension $n\\ge2$ so that $$\\label{grid:exponent-choice}\n 0.99k>p_0+p_1+1,\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{r=1}^{k-1}\\frac{r^{n-1}}{(n-1)!}<0.01.$$ Let $(\\zeta_j,h_j)$ be a sequence of pairs from a countable dense subset of an open disk in $\\mathbb C$ and the nonnegative integers, with every pair repeated infinitely often. At stage $j$, choose numbers $R_j\\ge1$, $c_j\\ge1$, an integer $N_j>h_j$, and a grid from Lemma <a href=\"#grid:polynomials\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"grid:polynomials\">4</a> sufficiently large and accurate for Lemma <a href=\"#grid:phases\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"grid:phases\">5</a> at order $h_j$. Set $$S_j=R_j e^{c_j/N_j},\\qquad \\log N_j\\le c_j/100.$$ Choose heights $D_j$ and positive numbers $\\alpha_j$ such that $$\\alpha_j^{-1}\\le e^{p_1c_j},$$ and, along the repetitions of every fixed pair, $$\\label{grid:height-rates}\n c_j\\longrightarrow\\infty,\\qquad\n D_j\\longrightarrow\\infty,\\qquad\n \\frac{D_j}{c_j}\\longrightarrow0.$$ There are phases $b_{j,\\xi}$, one per good projective direction and constant throughout its cycle of lifts, with the following property.*\n\n*Let $\\ell$ be a smooth real function on $\\mathbb C^n\\times\\mathbb C$, and let $$\\mathcal T=\\{(s,y,w):\\operatorname{Im}w>\\ell(s,y)\\}.$$ Suppose every stage satisfies these conditions:*\n\n\n1.  *On $|s|\\le S_j$, $$\\label{grid:height-gap}\n     \\ell(s,\\zeta_j)\\le D_j-2.$$*\n\n\n2.  *For every holomorphic $f$ on $\\mathcal T$ with $|f|\\le1$, every $w$ with $\\operatorname{Im}w=D_j$, and every $|s|=S_j$, $$\\label{grid:sphere-derivative}\n     |f_y(s,\\zeta_j,w)|\\le e^{p_0c_j}.$$*\n\n3.  *For every good direction $\\xi$, every lift $v=e^{2\\pi iq/N_j}v_\\xi$, and every $w$ on that height line, the holomorphic disk $$\\label{grid:sheared-disk}\n     t\\longmapsto\n     \\bigl(R_jv,\\ \\zeta_j+t,\\ w+i\\alpha_j b_{j,\\xi}t\\bigr),\n     \\qquad |t|<e^{m_0c_j},$$ is contained in $\\mathcal T$.*\n\n*Then every bounded holomorphic function on $\\mathcal T$ is constant. The phases depend only on the grids and their assigned jet orders, not on $\\ell$, $f$, or $w$.",
        "source_id": "PM-359.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-grid-criterion",

At stage $j$, choose numbers $R_j\\ge1$, $c_j\\ge1$, an integer $N_j>h_j$, and a grid from Lemma <a href=\"#grid:polynomials\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"grid:polynomials\">4</a> sufficiently large and accurate for Lemma <a href=\"#grid:phases\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"grid:phases\">5</a> at order $h_j$. Set $$S_j=R_j e^{c_j/N_j},\\qquad \\log N_j\\le c_j/100.$$ Choose heights $D_j$ and positive numbers $\\alpha_j$ such that $$\\alpha_j^{-1}\\le e^{p_1c_j},$$ and, along the repetitions of every fixed pair, $$\\label{grid:height-rates}\n c_j\\longrightarrow\\infty,\\qquad\n D_j\\longrightarrow\\infty,\\qquad\n \\frac{D_j}{c_j}\\longrightarrow0.$$ There are phases $b_{j,\\xi}$, one per good projective direction and constant throughout its cycle of lifts, with the following property.*\n\n*Let $\\ell$ be a smooth real function on $\\mathbb C^n\\times\\mathbb C$, and let $$\\mathcal T=\\{(s,y,w):\\operatorname{Im}w>\\ell(s,y)\\}.$$ Suppose every stage satisfies these conditions:*\n\n\n1.  *On $|s|\\le S_j$, $$\\label{grid:height-gap}\n     \\ell(s,\\zeta_j)\\le D_j-2.$$*\n\n\n2.  *For every holomorphic $f$ on $\\mathcal T$ with $|f|\\le1$, every $w$ with $\\operatorname{Im}w=D_j$, and every $|s|=S_j$, $$\\label{grid:sphere-derivative}\n     |f_y(s,\\zeta_j,w)|\\le e^{p_0c_j}.$$*\n\n3.  *For every good direction $\\xi$, every lift $v=e^{2\\pi iq/N_j}v_\\xi$, and every $w$ on that height line, the holomorphic disk $$\\label{grid:sheared-disk}\n     t\\longmapsto\n     \\bigl(R_jv,\\ \\zeta_j+t,\\ w+i\\alpha_j b_{j,\\xi}t\\bigr),\n     \\qquad |t|<e^{m_0c_j},$$ is contained in $\\mathcal T$.*\n\n*Then every bounded holomorphic function on $\\mathcal T$ is constant. The phases depend only on the grids and their assigned jet orders, not on $\\ell$, $f$, or $w$.",

      "exact_statement": "Fix constants $p_0,p_1\\ge0$ and $m_0>p_1$. Choose an integer $k$ and a finite dimension $n\\ge2$ so that $$\\label{grid:exponent-choice}\n 0.99k>p_0+p_1+1,\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{r=1}^{k-1}\\frac{r^{n-1}}{(n-1)!}<0.01.$$ Let $(\\zeta_j,h_j)$ be a sequence of pairs from a countable dense subset of an open disk in $\\mathbb C$ and the nonnegative integers, with every pair repeated infinitely often. At stage $j$, choose numbers $R_j\\ge1$, $c_j\\ge1$, an integer $N_j>h_j$, and a grid from Lemma <a href=\"#grid:polynomials\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"grid:polynomials\">4</a> sufficiently large and accurate for Lemma <a href=\"#grid:phases\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"grid:phases\">5</a> at order $h_j$. Set $$S_j=R_j e^{c_j/N_j},\\qquad \\log N_j\\le c_j/100.$$ Choose heights $D_j$ and positive numbers $\\alpha_j$ such that $$\\alpha_j^{-1}\\le e^{p_1c_j},$$ and, along the repetitions of every fixed pair, $$\\label{grid:height-rates}\n c_j\\longrightarrow\\infty,\\qquad\n D_j\\longrightarrow\\infty,\\qquad\n \\frac{D_j}{c_j}\\longrightarrow0.$$ There are phases $b_{j,\\xi}$, one per good projective direction and constant throughout its cycle of lifts, with the following property.*\n\n*Let $\\ell$ be a smooth real function on $\\mathbb C^n\\times\\mathbb C$, and let $$\\mathcal T=\\{(s,y,w):\\operatorname{Im}w>\\ell(s,y)\\}.$$ Suppose every stage satisfies these conditions:*\n\n\n1.  *On $|s|\\le S_j$, $$\\label{grid:height-gap}\n     \\ell(s,\\zeta_j)\\le D_j-2.$$*\n\n\n2.  *For every holomorphic $f$ on $\\mathcal T$ with $|f|\\le1$, every $w$ with $\\operatorname{Im}w=D_j$, and every $|s|=S_j$, $$\\label{grid:sphere-derivative}\n     |f_y(s,\\zeta_j,w)|\\le e^{p_0c_j}.$$*\n\n3.  *For every good direction $\\xi$, every lift $v=e^{2\\pi iq/N_j}v_\\xi$, and every $w$ on that height line, the holomorphic disk $$\\label{grid:sheared-disk}\n     t\\longmapsto\n     \\bigl(R_jv,\\ \\zeta_j+t,\\ w+i\\alpha_j b_{j,\\xi}t\\bigr),\n     \\qquad |t|<e^{m_0c_j},$$ is contained in $\\mathcal T$.*\n\n*Then every bounded holomorphic function on $\\mathcal T$ is constant. The phases depend only on the grids and their assigned jet orders, not on $\\ell$, $f$, or $w$.",
      "explanation": "If a smooth potential satisfies a height bound, a derivative bound on boundary spheres and containment of long sheared disks at the polynomial nodes at every stage, then every bounded holomorphic function on its tube is constant.",

        "anchor": "stmt-fiber-metric",
        "exact_quote": "Write $$P=\\tau q,\\qquad V=(B_{i\\bar j}),\\qquad\n G=(\\psi_{i\\bar j}),\\qquad h=(U_{i\\bar j}),\\qquad\n \\mathcal N=h_\\tau.$$ Where $h>0$, the complex Hessian of $\\ell_{\\mathrm{rad}}$ is $$\\label{fiber:metric-form}\n h_{i\\bar j}\\,ds_i\\,d\\bar s_j+\n P\\left|d\\log(y-\\zeta)+U_{\\tau i}\\,ds_i\\right|^2.$$ It is positive off the axis. Its spatial Schur complement is exactly $h$. The potential and its complex Hessian extend smoothly across $y=\\zeta$; on that axis the Hessian in the original splitting is $V\\oplus e^\\psi$, and is positive whenever $V>0$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-fiber-blocks",
        "exact_quote": "In tilted logarithmic coordinates, the tensor <a href=\"#fiber:curvature-convention\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"fiber:curvature-convention\">[fiber:curvature-convention]</a> has the following blocks: $$\\begin{aligned}\n A:=C_{0\\bar0 0\\bar0}\n   &=P^2P_{\\tau\\tau}-|\\partial P|_h^2,\n \\label{fiber:blockA}\\\\\n A_i:=C_{i\\bar0 0\\bar0}\n   &=P\\bigl(\\partial_iP_\\tau-\n       (\\mathcal N h^{-1}\\partial P)_i\\bigr),\n \\label{fiber:blockAi}\\\\\n W_{i\\bar j}:=C_{i\\bar j0\\bar0}\n   &=P\\bigl(\\partial_i\\partial_{\\bar j}\\log P\n       +P_\\tau\\mathcal N_{i\\bar j}\n       -P(\\mathcal N h^{-1}\\mathcal N)_{i\\bar j}\\bigr),\n \\label{fiber:blockW}\\\\\n C_{i\\bar0 k\\bar0}&=(\\nabla_h^{2,0}P)_{ik},\n \\label{fiber:blockspin}\\\\\n K_{ik\\bar j}:=C_{i\\bar j k\\bar0}\n   &=P\\nabla_i^h\\mathcal N_{k\\bar j}\n      +P_i\\mathcal N_{k\\bar j}+P_k\\mathcal N_{i\\bar j},\n \\label{fiber:blockK}\\\\\n L_{i\\bar j k\\bar l}:=C_{i\\bar j k\\bar l}\n   &=C^h_{i\\bar j k\\bar l}\n      +P(\\mathcal N_{i\\bar j}\\mathcal N_{k\\bar l}\n          +\\mathcal N_{i\\bar l}\\mathcal N_{k\\bar j}).\n \\label{fiber:blockL}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $C^h$ and $\\nabla^h$ are the curvature with sign <a href=\"#fiber:curvature-convention\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"fiber:curvature-convention\">[fiber:curvature-convention]</a> and the Kähler connection of the fixed-$\\tau$ spatial metric. Index positions in the products use the indicated Hermitian metric, with the usual conjugations.",
        "source_id": "PM-359.2"
      },

      "exact_statement": "In tilted logarithmic coordinates, the tensor <a href=\"#fiber:curvature-convention\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"fiber:curvature-convention\">[fiber:curvature-convention]</a> has the following blocks: $$\\begin{aligned}\n A:=C_{0\\bar0 0\\bar0}\n   &=P^2P_{\\tau\\tau}-|\\partial P|_h^2,\n \\label{fiber:blockA}\\\\\n A_i:=C_{i\\bar0 0\\bar0}\n   &=P\\bigl(\\partial_iP_\\tau-\n       (\\mathcal N h^{-1}\\partial P)_i\\bigr),\n \\label{fiber:blockAi}\\\\\n W_{i\\bar j}:=C_{i\\bar j0\\bar0}\n   &=P\\bigl(\\partial_i\\partial_{\\bar j}\\log P\n       +P_\\tau\\mathcal N_{i\\bar j}\n       -P(\\mathcal N h^{-1}\\mathcal N)_{i\\bar j}\\bigr),\n \\label{fiber:blockW}\\\\\n C_{i\\bar0 k\\bar0}&=(\\nabla_h^{2,0}P)_{ik},\n \\label{fiber:blockspin}\\\\\n K_{ik\\bar j}:=C_{i\\bar j k\\bar0}\n   &=P\\nabla_i^h\\mathcal N_{k\\bar j}\n      +P_i\\mathcal N_{k\\bar j}+P_k\\mathcal N_{i\\bar j},\n \\label{fiber:blockK}\\\\\n L_{i\\bar j k\\bar l}:=C_{i\\bar j k\\bar l}\n   &=C^h_{i\\bar j k\\bar l}\n      +P(\\mathcal N_{i\\bar j}\\mathcal N_{k\\bar l}\n          +\\mathcal N_{i\\bar l}\\mathcal N_{k\\bar j}).\n \\label{fiber:blockL}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $C^h$ and $\\nabla^h$ are the curvature with sign <a href=\"#fiber:curvature-convention\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"fiber:curvature-convention\">[fiber:curvature-convention]</a> and the Kähler connection of the fixed-$\\tau$ spatial metric.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-fiber-ranktwo",
        "exact_quote": "If $C(H,H)\\ge0$ for every Hermitian contravariant matrix $H$ of rank at most two, the underlying real sectional curvatures are nonpositive. Write the spatial, cross, and fiber diagonal blocks of $H$ as $T,b_\\times,t$. The contraction has the terms $$\\label{fiber:contraction}\n At^2+2t(W:T)+2W(b_\\times,\\bar b_\\times)+L(T,T),$$ and its other terms have absolute value at most $$\\label{fiber:otherterms}\n 2|\\nabla_h^{2,0}P(b_\\times,b_\\times)|\n +4|tA_i b_\\times^i|+4|(K:T)b_\\times|.$$ In particular the following quantitative criterion suffices. Suppose $A>0$, $W>0$, and a nonnegative quadratic form $\\mathcal R(T)$ satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n L(T,T)-\\frac{|W:T|^2}{.98A}&\\ge\\mathcal R(T),\n \\label{fiber:reserve}\\\\\n |\\nabla_h^{2,0}P(b,b)|&\\le\\tfrac14 W(b,\\bar b),\n \\label{fiber:spinabs}\\\\\n |A_i b^i|&\\le\\eta\\sqrt{A W(b,\\bar b)},\n \\label{fiber:Aiabs}\\\\\n |(K:T)b|&\\le\\eta\\sqrt{\\mathcal R(T)W(b,\\bar b)},\n \\label{fiber:Kabs}\n\\end{aligned}$$ for all such tests, where $\\eta\\le1/100$. Then $$\\label{fiber:retained}\n C(H,H)\\ge .01At^2+W(b_\\times,\\bar b_\\times)\n                         +\\tfrac12\\mathcal R(T).$$ All constants are independent of dimension.",
        "source_id": "PM-359.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-fiber-ranktwo",
          "exact_quote": "If $C(H,H)\\ge0$ for every Hermitian contravariant matrix $H$ of rank at most two, the underlying real sectional curvatures are nonpositive. The contraction has the terms $$\\label{fiber:contraction}\n At^2+2t(W:T)+2W(b_\\times,\\bar b_\\times)+L(T,T),$$ and its other terms have absolute value at most $$\\label{fiber:otherterms}\n 2|\\nabla_h^{2,0}P(b_\\times,b_\\times)|\n +4|tA_i b_\\times^i|+4|(K:T)b_\\times|.$$ In particular the following quantitative criterion suffices. Suppose $A>0$, $W>0$, and a nonnegative quadratic form $\\mathcal R(T)$ satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n L(T,T)-\\frac{|W:T|^2}{.98A}&\\ge\\mathcal R(T),\n \\label{fiber:reserve}\\\\\n |\\nabla_h^{2,0}P(b,b)|&\\le\\tfrac14 W(b,\\bar b),\n \\label{fiber:spinabs}\\\\\n |A_i b^i|&\\le\\eta\\sqrt{A W(b,\\bar b)},\n \\label{fiber:Aiabs}\\\\\n |(K:T)b|&\\le\\eta\\sqrt{\\mathcal R(T)W(b,\\bar b)},\n \\label{fiber:Kabs}\n\\end{aligned}$$ for all such tests, where $\\eta\\le1/100$. Then $$\\label{fiber:retained}\n C(H,H)\\ge .01At^2+W(b_\\times,\\bar b_\\times)\n                         +\\tfrac12\\mathcal R(T).$$ All constants are independent of dimension.",
          "source_id": "PM-359.2"

      "exact_statement": "If $C(H,H)\\ge0$ for every Hermitian contravariant matrix $H$ of rank at most two, the underlying real sectional curvatures are nonpositive. Suppose $A>0$, $W>0$, and a nonnegative quadratic form $\\mathcal R(T)$ satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n L(T,T)-\\frac{|W:T|^2}{.98A}&\\ge\\mathcal R(T),\n \\label{fiber:reserve}\\\\\n |\\nabla_h^{2,0}P(b,b)|&\\le\\tfrac14 W(b,\\bar b),\n \\label{fiber:spinabs}\\\\\n |A_i b^i|&\\le\\eta\\sqrt{A W(b,\\bar b)},\n \\label{fiber:Aiabs}\\\\\n |(K:T)b|&\\le\\eta\\sqrt{\\mathcal R(T)W(b,\\bar b)},\n \\label{fiber:Kabs}\n\\end{aligned}$$ for all such tests, where $\\eta\\le1/100$. Then $$\\label{fiber:retained}\n C(H,H)\\ge .01At^2+W(b_\\times,\\bar b_\\times)\n                         +\\tfrac12\\mathcal R(T).$$ All constants are independent of dimension.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-fiber-perturbation",
        "exact_quote": "In any fixed holomorphic coordinates let $H$ denote the cubic jet viewed as a map from two tangent inputs to a covector, and write $H=\\Gamma g$. For a perturbation with $g+\\Delta g>0$, the change of the cubic contraction $Hg^{-1}H^*$ is exactly $$\\label{fiber:perturbformula}\n \\Gamma\\Delta H^*+\\Delta H\\Gamma^*-\\Gamma\\Delta g\\Gamma^*\n +(\\Delta H-\\Gamma\\Delta g)(g+\\Delta g)^{-1}\n                 (\\Delta H-\\Gamma\\Delta g)^*.$$ Consequently, if $\\|g^{-1}\\Delta g\\|$ in metric-unit axes is at most one half, the inverse of the perturbed metric is at most twice that of $g$, and $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{fiber:perturbbound}\n \\|\\Delta C\\|\\le {}&\\|\\Delta j^4\\ell\\|\n       +2\\|\\Gamma\\|\\|\\Delta H\\|\n       +\\|\\Gamma\\|^2\\|\\Delta g\\|\\\\\n &+2\\|g^{-1}\\|\n       (\\|\\Delta H\\|+\\|\\Gamma\\|\\|\\Delta g\\|)^2.\n \\notag\n\\end{aligned}$$ The same bound, with an absolute factor, controls rank-two contractions.",
        "source_id": "PM-359.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-fiber-perturbation",
          "exact_quote": "In any fixed holomorphic coordinates let $H$ denote the cubic jet viewed as a map from two tangent inputs to a covector, and write $H=\\Gamma g$. For a perturbation with $g+\\Delta g>0$, the change of the cubic contraction $Hg^{-1}H^*$ is exactly $$\\label{fiber:perturbformula}\n \\Gamma\\Delta H^*+\\Delta H\\Gamma^*-\\Gamma\\Delta g\\Gamma^*\n +(\\Delta H-\\Gamma\\Delta g)(g+\\Delta g)^{-1}\n                 (\\Delta H-\\Gamma\\Delta g)^*.$$ Consequently, if $\\|g^{-1}\\Delta g\\|$ in metric-unit axes is at most one half, the inverse of the perturbed metric is at most twice that of $g$, and $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{fiber:perturbbound}\n \\|\\Delta C\\|\\le {}&\\|\\Delta j^4\\ell\\|\n       +2\\|\\Gamma\\|\\|\\Delta H\\|\n       +\\|\\Gamma\\|^2\\|\\Delta g\\|\\\\\n &+2\\|g^{-1}\\|\n       (\\|\\Delta H\\|+\\|\\Gamma\\|\\|\\Delta g\\|)^2.\n \\notag\n\\end{aligned}$$ The same bound, with an absolute factor, controls rank-two contractions.",
          "source_id": "PM-359.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "In any fixed holomorphic coordinates let $H$ denote the cubic jet viewed as a map from two tangent inputs to a covector, and write $H=\\Gamma g$. For a perturbation with $g+\\Delta g>0$, the change of the cubic contraction $Hg^{-1}H^*$ is exactly $$\\label{fiber:perturbformula}\n \\Gamma\\Delta H^*+\\Delta H\\Gamma^*-\\Gamma\\Delta g\\Gamma^*\n +(\\Delta H-\\Gamma\\Delta g)(g+\\Delta g)^{-1}\n                 (\\Delta H-\\Gamma\\Delta g)^*.$$ Consequently, if $\\|g^{-1}\\Delta g\\|$ in metric-unit axes is at most one half, the inverse of the perturbed metric is at most twice that of $g$, and $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{fiber:perturbbound}\n \\|\\Delta C\\|\\le {}&\\|\\Delta j^4\\ell\\|\n       +2\\|\\Gamma\\|\\|\\Delta H\\|\n       +\\|\\Gamma\\|^2\\|\\Delta g\\|\\\\\n &+2\\|g^{-1}\\|\n       (\\|\\Delta H\\|+\\|\\Gamma\\|\\|\\Delta g\\|)^2.\n \\notag\n\\end{aligned}$$ The same bound, with an absolute factor, controls rank-two contractions.",
      "explanation": "The change of the cubic contraction under a metric perturbation has an exact algebraic expression, which yields operator-norm bounds on the change of the curvature tensor.",

      "hypotheses": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-quiet-scalar",
        "exact_quote": "Put $$q_a=(1+e^Eu)^{b_0},\\quad r=r_q=q_x/q,\\quad\n D=D_q=(q_x+q_{xx})/q=r(1+r)+r_x,\\quad e_0=q_E/q.$$ For all sufficiently large $E$ and all $\\tau>0$, $$\\begin{gathered}\n q\\ge1,\\quad q_x>0,\\quad\n D\\ge r(r+\\tfrac12),\\quad q_{xx}/q_x\\ge-\\tfrac1{16},\n \\label{quiet:slopes}\\\\\n 0<r\\le C,\\quad |e_0|+|q_{EE}/q|\\le C,\n \\quad |r_E|\\le Cr.\n \\label{quiet:derivatives}\n\\end{gathered}$$ For $\\tau\\le1$, the first two quantities in the middle of <a href=\"#quiet:derivatives\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"quiet:derivatives\">[quiet:derivatives]</a> are also at most $Cr$. For $1\\le j\\le4$, $$\\label{quiet:Fderivatives}\n |\\partial_E^jF|+|\\partial_E^j\\bar F|\\le C,\n \\qquad \\bar F(\\tau,E)=\\tau^{-1}\\int_0^\\tau F(\\log v,E)\\,dv.$$ For $0\\le j\\le4$ and $\\tau\\ge e^{-E/4}$, $$\\label{quiet:difference}\n |\\partial_E^j(F-\\bar F)|\\le C e^{-E/4},\\qquad\n |\\partial_E^j(F-\\bar F)|\\le Cr.$$ The following additional estimates will be used: $$\\begin{aligned}\n q_x&\\ge e^E &&(e^{-E/4}\\le\\tau\\le e^E),\n \\label{quiet:intermediate}\\\\\n q&\\le C(e^{b_0E}+\\log(1+\\tau)),\\qquad\n q_x\\ge c(1+e^{b_0E}(1+\\tau)^{-1/16}) &&(\\tau\\ge1),\n \\label{quiet:tail}\\\\\n r&\\ge .9 b_0(1+\\tau)^{-1/16}\n              &&(1\\le\\tau\\le e^{3b_0E}),\n \\label{quiet:middle-slope}\\\\\n q&\\le C,\\quad q-1\\le Ce^E\\tau,\\quad\n r\\asymp e^E\\tau,\\quad D\\asymp e^E\\tau\n              &&(0<\\tau\\le2e^{-E}).\n \\label{quiet:axis-slope}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular $q_x\\ge c$ for $\\tau\\ge e^{-E}$, and $q^{-1}\\le Cr$ for $\\tau\\ge2e^{-E}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-359.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-quiet-scalar",
          "exact_quote": "Put $$q_a=(1+e^Eu)^{b_0},\\quad r=r_q=q_x/q,\\quad\n D=D_q=(q_x+q_{xx})/q=r(1+r)+r_x,\\quad e_0=q_E/q.$$ For all sufficiently large $E$ and all $\\tau>0$, $$\\begin{gathered}\n q\\ge1,\\quad q_x>0,\\quad\n D\\ge r(r+\\tfrac12),\\quad q_{xx}/q_x\\ge-\\tfrac1{16},\n \\label{quiet:slopes}\\\\\n 0<r\\le C,\\quad |e_0|+|q_{EE}/q|\\le C,\n \\quad |r_E|\\le Cr.\n \\label{quiet:derivatives}\n\\end{gathered}$$ For $\\tau\\le1$, the first two quantities in the middle of <a href=\"#quiet:derivatives\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"quiet:derivatives\">[quiet:derivatives]</a> are also at most $Cr$. For $1\\le j\\le4$, $$\\label{quiet:Fderivatives}\n |\\partial_E^jF|+|\\partial_E^j\\bar F|\\le C,\n \\qquad \\bar F(\\tau,E)=\\tau^{-1}\\int_0^\\tau F(\\log v,E)\\,dv.$$ For $0\\le j\\le4$ and $\\tau\\ge e^{-E/4}$, $$\\label{quiet:difference}\n |\\partial_E^j(F-\\bar F)|\\le C e^{-E/4},\\qquad\n |\\partial_E^j(F-\\bar F)|\\le Cr.$$ The following additional estimates will be used: $$\\begin{aligned}\n q_x&\\ge e^E &&(e^{-E/4}\\le\\tau\\le e^E),\n \\label{quiet:intermediate}\\\\\n q&\\le C(e^{b_0E}+\\log(1+\\tau)),\\qquad\n q_x\\ge c(1+e^{b_0E}(1+\\tau)^{-1/16}) &&(\\tau\\ge1),\n \\label{quiet:tail}\\\\\n r&\\ge .9 b_0(1+\\tau)^{-1/16}\n              &&(1\\le\\tau\\le e^{3b_0E}),\n \\label{quiet:middle-slope}\\\\\n q&\\le C,\\quad q-1\\le Ce^E\\tau,\\quad\n r\\asymp e^E\\tau,\\quad D\\asymp e^E\\tau\n              &&(0<\\tau\\le2e^{-E}).\n \\label{quiet:axis-slope}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular $q_x\\ge c$ for $\\tau\\ge e^{-E}$, and $q^{-1}\\le Cr$ for $\\tau\\ge2e^{-E}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-359.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Put $$q_a=(1+e^Eu)^{b_0},\\quad r=r_q=q_x/q,\\quad\n D=D_q=(q_x+q_{xx})/q=r(1+r)+r_x,\\quad e_0=q_E/q.$$ For all sufficiently large $E$ and all $\\tau>0$, $$\\begin{gathered}\n q\\ge1,\\quad q_x>0,\\quad\n D\\ge r(r+\\tfrac12),\\quad q_{xx}/q_x\\ge-\\tfrac1{16},\n \\label{quiet:slopes}\\\\\n 0<r\\le C,\\quad |e_0|+|q_{EE}/q|\\le C,\n \\quad |r_E|\\le Cr.\n \\label{quiet:derivatives}\n\\end{gathered}$$ For $\\tau\\le1$, the first two quantities in the middle of <a href=\"#quiet:derivatives\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"quiet:derivatives\">[quiet:derivatives]</a> are also at most $Cr$. For $1\\le j\\le4$, $$\\label{quiet:Fderivatives}\n |\\partial_E^jF|+|\\partial_E^j\\bar F|\\le C,\n \\qquad \\bar F(\\tau,E)=\\tau^{-1}\\int_0^\\tau F(\\log v,E)\\,dv.$$ For $0\\le j\\le4$ and $\\tau\\ge e^{-E/4}$, $$\\label{quiet:difference}\n |\\partial_E^j(F-\\bar F)|\\le C e^{-E/4},\\qquad\n |\\partial_E^j(F-\\bar F)|\\le Cr.$$ The following additional estimates will be used: $$\\begin{aligned}\n q_x&\\ge e^E &&(e^{-E/4}\\le\\tau\\le e^E),\n \\label{quiet:intermediate}\\\\\n q&\\le C(e^{b_0E}+\\log(1+\\tau)),\\qquad\n q_x\\ge c(1+e^{b_0E}(1+\\tau)^{-1/16}) &&(\\tau\\ge1),\n \\label{quiet:tail}\\\\\n r&\\ge .9 b_0(1+\\tau)^{-1/16}\n              &&(1\\le\\tau\\le e^{3b_0E}),\n \\label{quiet:middle-slope}\\\\\n q&\\le C,\\quad q-1\\le Ce^E\\tau,\\quad\n r\\asymp e^E\\tau,\\quad D\\asymp e^E\\tau\n              &&(0<\\tau\\le2e^{-E}).\n \\label{quiet:axis-slope}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular $q_x\\ge c$ for $\\tau\\ge e^{-E}$, and $q^{-1}\\le Cr$ for $\\tau\\ge2e^{-E}$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-quiet-profile",
        "exact_quote": "There exist positive constants $\\epsilon,\\mu$ and a lower threshold $E_{\\min}$, depending on the fixed profile constants but not on $n$, with the following property. Let $E$ be a smooth spatial function and use $q=q_0(x,E(s))$ with constant center and no shear. At a spatial point suppose, in some fixed holomorphic affine coordinates, that:*\n\n1.  *$G\\ge V>0$. The operator norms of $V,G$, their inverses, their first and second metric jets, and all derivatives of $E$ of orders one through four are at most $e^{\\epsilon E}$.*\n\n2.  *For every Hermitian contravariant matrix $T$ of rank at most two, $C^V(T,T)\\ge e^{-\\epsilon E}|T|^2$, with the norm in those affine coordinates.*\n\n3.  *In $G$-unit axes, $$|\\partial E|_G^2\\le\\mu,\\qquad\n     \\|\\partial\\bar\\partial E\\|_G\\le\\mu,\\qquad\n     \\|\\nabla_h^{2,0}E\\|_G\\le\\mu\n     \\quad\\hbox{for every }\\tau>0.$$*\n\n\n\n*If $E\\ge E_{\\min}$, the resulting metric is positive for every $y$ over that spatial point and has nonpositive real sectional curvature there. Uniformly in $\\tau$, $$\\label{quiet:schur}\n \\mathcal N=(1+O(\\mu))G,\\qquad\n h\\asymp V+\\tau G.$$ More precisely, off the fiber axis the rank-two curvature calculation retains fixed positive multiples of $$\\label{quiet:margins}\n At^2+W(b_\\times,\\bar b_\\times)+P|T|_{\\mathcal N}^2\n +\\boldsymbol{1}_{\\{\\tau\\le e^{-E/4}\\}}\n                          e^{-\\epsilon E}|T|^2.$$ Here the fiber blocks use the tilted logarithmic coordinates, $|T|_{\\mathcal N}$ is its Frobenius norm in $\\mathcal N$-unit spatial axes, and $\\boldsymbol{1}$ denotes the indicated indicator. The meaning of the final hypothesis is noncircular: positivity of $h$ follows from the preceding hypotheses and does not use $\\nabla_h^{2,0}E$.",
        "source_id": "PM-359.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-quiet-shear",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#quiet:profile\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"quiet:profile\">15</a>, suppose $E$ and the center are constant on the spatial region under consideration. Assume also that the first and pure second spatial derivatives of $\\psi$, in the affine coordinates of that proposition, have norms at most $e^{\\epsilon E}$. Add $$\\ell_{\\mathrm{sh}}=\\operatorname{Re}(c_{\\mathrm{sh}}(s)(y-\\zeta)).$$ On the support of its coefficient’s derivatives suppose $\\psi\\ge-J$, where $J\\ge0$, and $$\\label{quiet:shear-small}\n \\|j^{\\le4}c_{\\mathrm{sh}}\\|\\le e^{-C E-J/2}.$$ For a sufficiently large $C$ depending only on the profile and the constants in the quiet criterion, this perturbation preserves metric positivity and nonpositive real sectional curvature for all $y$. Moreover its metric is at least one half the unperturbed metric, and fixed fractions of the curvature margins remain. The choice of $C$ is independent of dimension.",
        "source_id": "PM-359.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-radial-odes",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $a,a_\\psi,Y>0$, $\\lambda\\ge1$, and $$\\label{radial:kappa-assumptions}\n \\frac1{1+Y}\\le\\kappa\\le1.1,\\qquad\n \\frac{d\\kappa}{d\\log Y}\\ge-\\kappa.$$ Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{radial:clock-equations}\n \\frac{d\\nu}{du}&=\\frac1Y,&\n \\frac{d\\log Y}{du}&=\\kappa(1+1/Y),&\n \\frac{d\\log\\lambda}{du}&=1-p,\\\\\n \\frac{d\\Psi}{du}&=\\frac1H,&\n \\frac{d\\log H}{du}&=\\kappa(1+1/Y)-p-1/Y.\\notag\n\\end{aligned}$$ If $Y\\ge t$ initially, then $Y\\ge t$ subsequently. With smooth bounded controls $p,\\kappa$ on each finite $u$ interval, positive solutions have no blowup on that interval. A cap of the form $\\kappa=C_\\kappa/(1+Y)$, with fixed $C_\\kappa\\ge1$, has no blowup at finite $\\nu$ for the prescribed bounded controls.*\n\n\n*If $p=1$, then $$\\label{radial:H-logY}\n \\frac{d\\log H}{d\\log Y}=1-\\frac1\\kappa.$$ In particular, $p=\\kappa=1$ holds both $\\lambda$ and $H$ constant. If $\\kappa\\ge1$ on an interval beginning at $u_0$, then the total increase of $\\nu$ on that interval is at most $1/Y(u_0)$, even if its finite $u$ length is arbitrarily large.",
        "source_id": "PM-359.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-radial-odes",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $a,a_\\psi,Y>0$, $\\lambda\\ge1$, and $$\\label{radial:kappa-assumptions}\n \\frac1{1+Y}\\le\\kappa\\le1.1,\\qquad\n \\frac{d\\kappa}{d\\log Y}\\ge-\\kappa.$$ Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{radial:clock-equations}\n \\frac{d\\nu}{du}&=\\frac1Y,&\n \\frac{d\\log Y}{du}&=\\kappa(1+1/Y),&\n \\frac{d\\log\\lambda}{du}&=1-p,\\\\\n \\frac{d\\Psi}{du}&=\\frac1H,&\n \\frac{d\\log H}{du}&=\\kappa(1+1/Y)-p-1/Y.\\notag\n\\end{aligned}$$ If $Y\\ge t$ initially, then $Y\\ge t$ subsequently. With smooth bounded controls $p,\\kappa$ on each finite $u$ interval, positive solutions have no blowup on that interval. A cap of the form $\\kappa=C_\\kappa/(1+Y)$, with fixed $C_\\kappa\\ge1$, has no blowup at finite $\\nu$ for the prescribed bounded controls.*\n\n\n*If $p=1$, then $$\\label{radial:H-logY}\n \\frac{d\\log H}{d\\log Y}=1-\\frac1\\kappa.$$ In particular, $p=\\kappa=1$ holds both $\\lambda$ and $H$ constant.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-radial-scaled",
        "exact_quote": "Assume <a href=\"#radial:kappa-assumptions\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"radial:kappa-assumptions\">[radial:kappa-assumptions]</a> and $\\lambda\\ge1$. Suppose $Y\\ge1$, $Y\\ge t$, the first two derivatives of $\\kappa$ in $\\log Y$ are bounded by a fixed constant, and $$\\label{radial:p-close}\n |p-1|+\\left|\\frac{dp}{d\\log Y}\\right|\n      +\\left|\\frac{d^2p}{d(\\log Y)^2}\\right|\\le\\delta,\n \\qquad 0<\\delta\\le\\tfrac1{10}.$$ At a point use $G_0$-unit affine axes divided by $\\sqrt H$. In these axes $G_0=H^{-1}I$ at the point. The transverse and radial Euclidean axis lengths are $$\\label{radial:axis-lengths}\n \\sqrt{\\frac tY},\\qquad\n \\sqrt{\\frac{t}{Y(1+pY)}}.$$ In these fixed coordinates:*\n\n1.  *derivatives of $\\Psi$ and $B$ in orders one through four are bounded by $C/H$ and $C\\lambda/H$, respectively;*\n\n2.  *metric jets through order two of $V-\\lambda G_0$, with $\\lambda$ frozen at its value at the point, are bounded by $C\\delta\\lambda/H$;*\n\n3.  *derivatives of $u$ in orders one through four are bounded by $C$;*\n\n4.  *if a further potential has Euclidean derivatives in orders two through four bounded by $D_e$, its Hessian and first two metric jets here are bounded by $CD_e/(a_\\psi H)$.*\n\n*All constants are independent of dimension.",
        "source_id": "PM-359.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-radial-scaled",
          "exact_quote": "Assume <a href=\"#radial:kappa-assumptions\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"radial:kappa-assumptions\">[radial:kappa-assumptions]</a> and $\\lambda\\ge1$. Suppose $Y\\ge1$, $Y\\ge t$, the first two derivatives of $\\kappa$ in $\\log Y$ are bounded by a fixed constant, and $$\\label{radial:p-close}\n |p-1|+\\left|\\frac{dp}{d\\log Y}\\right|\n      +\\left|\\frac{d^2p}{d(\\log Y)^2}\\right|\\le\\delta,\n \\qquad 0<\\delta\\le\\tfrac1{10}.$$ At a point use $G_0$-unit affine axes divided by $\\sqrt H$. In these axes $G_0=H^{-1}I$ at the point. The transverse and radial Euclidean axis lengths are $$\\label{radial:axis-lengths}\n \\sqrt{\\frac tY},\\qquad\n \\sqrt{\\frac{t}{Y(1+pY)}}.$$ In these fixed coordinates:*\n\n1.  *derivatives of $\\Psi$ and $B$ in orders one through four are bounded by $C/H$ and $C\\lambda/H$, respectively;*\n\n2.  *metric jets through order two of $V-\\lambda G_0$, with $\\lambda$ frozen at its value at the point, are bounded by $C\\delta\\lambda/H$;*\n\n3.  *derivatives of $u$ in orders one through four are bounded by $C$;*\n\n4.  *if a further potential has Euclidean derivatives in orders two through four bounded by $D_e$, its Hessian and first two metric jets here are bounded by $CD_e/(a_\\psi H)$.*\n\n*All constants are independent of dimension.",
          "source_id": "PM-359.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Assume <a href=\"#radial:kappa-assumptions\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"radial:kappa-assumptions\">[radial:kappa-assumptions]</a> and $\\lambda\\ge1$. Suppose $Y\\ge1$, $Y\\ge t$, the first two derivatives of $\\kappa$ in $\\log Y$ are bounded by a fixed constant, and $$\\label{radial:p-close}\n |p-1|+\\left|\\frac{dp}{d\\log Y}\\right|\n      +\\left|\\frac{d^2p}{d(\\log Y)^2}\\right|\\le\\delta,\n \\qquad 0<\\delta\\le\\tfrac1{10}.$$ At a point use $G_0$-unit affine axes divided by $\\sqrt H$. In these axes $G_0=H^{-1}I$ at the point. The transverse and radial Euclidean axis lengths are $$\\label{radial:axis-lengths}\n \\sqrt{\\frac tY},\\qquad\n \\sqrt{\\frac{t}{Y(1+pY)}}.$$ In these fixed coordinates:*\n\n1.  *derivatives of $\\Psi$ and $B$ in orders one through four are bounded by $C/H$ and $C\\lambda/H$, respectively;*\n\n2.  *metric jets through order two of $V-\\lambda G_0$, with $\\lambda$ frozen at its value at the point, are bounded by $C\\delta\\lambda/H$;*\n\n3.  *derivatives of $u$ in orders one through four are bounded by $C$;*\n\n4.  *if a further potential has Euclidean derivatives in orders two through four bounded by $D_e$, its Hessian and first two metric jets here are bounded by $CD_e/(a_\\psi H)$.*\n\n*All constants are independent of dimension.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-radial-aligned",
        "exact_quote": "There is an absolute $\\delta_0>0$ with the following property. Fix $\\sigma>0$ and take the severity $E$ sufficiently large in terms of $\\sigma$ and the fixed profile. Assume the hypotheses of Lemma <a href=\"#radial:scaled\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"radial:scaled\">19</a>, with $\\delta\\le\\delta_0$, and also $$\\tfrac12\\le\\kappa\\le1.1,\\qquad H\\ge\\sigma,\n \\qquad G=G_0+G_e,\\qquad\n |\\partial^{[j]}G_e|\\le\\delta/H\\quad(0\\le j\\le2)$$ in its scaled affine axes. Here $G=\\partial\\bar\\partial\\psi$ and $\\partial^{[j]}$ denotes any real or complex metric jet of order $j$, measured as a multilinear operator. Suppose either $$\\label{radial:aligned-alternatives}\n \\lambda=1,\\qquad\\text{or}\\qquad\n H\\ge M_E(1+\\log\\lambda)^2,$$ where $M_E$ is sufficiently large depending only on the fixed profile and $E$. Then the constant-center fiber potential defines a positive metric with nonpositive real sectional curvature. Its spatial Schur complement satisfies $h\\ge cV$.*\n\n\n*More precisely, in scaled spatial axes and the tilted logarithmic fiber coordinate of Lemma <a href=\"#fiber:blocks\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"fiber:blocks\">11</a>, the contraction of its negative curvature on each Hermitian rank-at-most-two matrix with blocks $(T,b,t_f)$ is bounded below by $$\\label{radial:aligned-margin}\n c\\left(A t_f^2+W(b,\\bar b)\n               +\\frac{\\lambda+\\tau}{H}|T|^2\n               +P|T|_G^2\\right),$$ where $c>0$ can be fixed independently of dimension and the stage parameters once the stated thresholds hold. The coefficient $A$ and the form $W$ are those of Lemma <a href=\"#fiber:blocks\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"fiber:blocks\">11</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-359.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-center-change",
        "exact_quote": "Choose the profile constants in the following order: first the fixed exponent $b_0$ of the quiet profile; then a sufficiently large absolute constant $d_0$; and then $K_0$ sufficiently large in terms of $d_0$ and absolute constants specified in the proof. Fix $E$ and old data as in <a href=\"#center:old-data\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"center:old-data\">[center:old-data]</a>. There is a width parameter $L$, chosen using only these data and the fixed profile, such that the following construction is possible.*\n\n\n*After choosing $L$, choose a sufficiently large $\\lambda$, and then a sufficiently large $H$; explicitly one can require $$\\label{center:target-order}\n \\lambda\\ge\\max\\{e^{3L},\\Lambda_f\\},\n \\qquad H\\ge M_f(1+\\log\\lambda)^2,$$ where $\\Lambda_f,M_f$ are finite constants determined by the fixed profile, $E,L$, the old data, and $\\delta$, but not by $\\lambda,H$. Over three fixed units of the variable $\\Psi$, one can change from any prescribed constant center in the unit disk to any other such center, returning to the original profile $q_0(x,E)$ at the end. The final twist differs from the old twist by a nonnegative constant, possibly very large.*\n\n*During the change the potential is smooth, its metric is positive and has nonpositive real sectional curvature, and the metric bounds $cV$ from below on spatial projection, for a fixed $c>0$. There is no restriction on the cost of the final constant offset. All power exponents and smallness tolerances used before choosing the spatial dimension are dimension independent.",
        "source_id": "PM-359.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-assembly-base",
        "exact_quote": "For some fixed finite integer $n$, there is a smooth strictly plurisubharmonic potential $\\ell$ on $\\mathbb C^n\\times\\mathbb C$ whose Kähler metric is complete and has nonpositive real sectional curvature.         "source_id": "PM-359.2"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-assembly-well-bounds",
        "exact_quote": "There is an absolute exponent $C_{\\rm pol}$ such that, for $t\\geq1$ and $1\\leq r\\leq4$, $$\\label{assembly:well-raw}\n |\\partial^{[r]}W_j|\n \\leq C(n,d)\\exp\\{C_{\\rm pol}(\\max(l,0)+c+\\log N)\\}.$$ There is a constant $L_n<\\infty$ such that, after increasing the degree threshold, $$\\label{assembly:well-tail}\n |\\partial^{[r]}W_j|\\leq N^{L_n}\n \\qquad(1\\leq r\\leq4,\\ l\\geq L_n\\log N).$$ Both statements hold throughout the indicated outer ranges.",
        "source_id": "PM-359.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-assembly-well-bounds",
          "exact_quote": "There is an absolute exponent $C_{\\rm pol}$ such that, for $t\\geq1$ and $1\\leq r\\leq4$, $$\\label{assembly:well-raw}\n |\\partial^{[r]}W_j|\n \\leq C(n,d)\\exp\\{C_{\\rm pol}(\\max(l,0)+c+\\log N)\\}.$$ There is a constant $L_n<\\infty$ such that, after increasing the degree threshold, $$\\label{assembly:well-tail}\n |\\partial^{[r]}W_j|\\leq N^{L_n}\n \\qquad(1\\leq r\\leq4,\\ l\\geq L_n\\log N).$$ Both statements hold throughout the indicated outer ranges.",
          "source_id": "PM-359.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is an absolute exponent $C_{\\rm pol}$ such that, for $t\\geq1$ and $1\\leq r\\leq4$, $$\\label{assembly:well-raw}\n |\\partial^{[r]}W_j|\n \\leq C(n,d)\\exp\\{C_{\\rm pol}(\\max(l,0)+c+\\log N)\\}.$$ There is a constant $L_n<\\infty$ such that, after increasing the degree threshold, $$\\label{assembly:well-tail}\n |\\partial^{[r]}W_j|\\leq N^{L_n}\n \\qquad(1\\leq r\\leq4,\\ l\\geq L_n\\log N).$$ Both statements hold throughout the indicated outer ranges.",
      "explanation": "The logarithmic polynomial wells have jets bounded by an exponential in the parameters with an absolute exponent, and by a power of the degree far from the origin.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **holomorphic Liouville property** ([source](#sec:introduction)): We say that $M$ has the *holomorphic Liouville property* when $H^\infty(M)=\mathbb C$.
- **negative Hermitian curvature tensor** ([source](#fiber:curvature-convention)): Hereafter $C=-R$ denotes the negative Hermitian curvature tensor:
- **profile function u** ([source](#sec:quiet)): Define a positive function $u$ of $\tau>0$ by
- **severity** ([source](#sec:quiet)): For a real parameter $E$, called the severity, set
- **radial clocks** ([source](#pm-h-14)): Write $t=\|s\|^2$, $\nu=\log t$, and let $B$ and $\Psi$ be radial potentials.
- **aligned** ([source](#sec:radial)): Here *aligned* means that their metric jets through order two are close to proportional, with the scalar frozen at the evaluation point.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-main-sectional-bound | real sectional curvature of g | ≤ | $-1$ | all real two-planes |
| q-model-sectional | model sectional curvature | = | $-1-3\beta^2$ | orthonormal real pairs with complex pairing of modulus beta |
| q-zero-count | number of projective zeros M_N | = | $N^{n-1}$ | degree N, dimension n |
| q-shell-exponent | separation exponent k_* | = | $30$ | absolute choice |
| q-dimension-condition | sum of low-degree evaluation ratios | < | $\frac1{100}$ | fixed integers k and n |
| q-exponent-choice | 0.99k | > | $p_0+p_1+1$ | integer k |
| q-sphere-derivative | derivative of f in y on the sphere | ≤ | $e^{p_0c_j}$ | points at the testing height on the sphere of stage j |
| q-sheared-radius | radius of the sheared disk | < | $e^{m_0c_j}$ | stage j |
| q-height-gap | potential on the center axis | ≤ | $D_j-2$ | spatial radius up to S_j |
| q-rank-norm | contraction of a form with a low-rank test matrix | ≤ | $\sqrt2\\|Q\\|\,\|T\|$ | independent of dimension |
| q-kappa-range | clock control kappa | ≤ | $1.1$ | positive Y |
| q-test-exponent-m0 | exponent m_0 | = | $d/2$ | depth parameter d |
| q-kappa-star | accelerated control kappa_* | = | $1.08$ | fixed constant |
